產業速報  
                                        2026 年 10 月 21 日

1 群益投顧 
台玻 (1802 TT ) 10/20/2026 首戰台灣電路板產業國際博覽會 (TPCA Show 2026) 。本次
會 中 主 要 展 出 其 高 階 玻 纖 布 。 當 前 以 Low Dk1/Dk2 為 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 主 要 營 收來源。
Low CTE 則已於 2025 年 底 開 始 小 量 出 貨 ， 目 前 產 品 出 貨 量 已 較2026 年 初 顯 著 增 加 。
當前營收比重為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE 。鑒於 Low Dk 1/Dk2 產 品 出 貨 動 能 佳，
Low CTE 營 收 貢 獻 逐步提升下，毛利表現亦將持續走揚。產品供需結構尚處緊缺階
段， 公司 亦 有 望 部 分 反 映 報 價 。 給 予 投 資 建 議Buy ， 目 標 價 81 元。

研 究 員 ： 蔡 季 衡   
A52320@cim.capital.com.tw

台 玻 首 戰 TPCA Show ：  
台玻 10/20/2026 首戰 台 灣 電 路 板 產 業 國 際 博 覽 會(TPCA Show 2026) 。本次
會中 主 要 展 出 其 高 階 玻 纖 布 ， 包 括 TD(Low Dk1) 、 TDA(Low Dk2) 、 TS(Low 
CTE) 、 Q (Q 布 )等 ， 並 詳 解 各 產 品 特 性 及 運 用 範 疇 。 
一、 聚 焦 於 Low Dk 、 Low CTE 玻纖布 ：  
TD(Low Dk1) 、 TDA(Low Dk2) 具 低 介 電 損 耗 並 於 維 持 一 定 電 性 水 準 下 ， 提
供 較 佳 產 品 穩 定 性 。 目 前 主 要 應 用 於 電 子 產 業 及PCB 產 業 、IC 載 板 、 先 進
封 裝 材 料 、 航 空 及 汽 車 領 域 等 。 
TS(Low CTE) 具 低 熱 膨 脹 係 數 特 性， 並 具 備 高機 械 韌 性(剛性 )、 絕 佳 電 性 絕
緣 及 先 進 樹 酯兼 容 性。目 前 主 要 應 用 於 5G/6G 、微 波 通 訊 設 備、高 速 電 路 板、
高頻 PCB 、 光 模 塊 、 高 速 交 換 器 及資 料 傳 輸 等。  
二 、 目 前 營 收 比 重 為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE ：  
E 布 轉 至 中 國 產 銷 ，以 Low Dk 1/Dk2 為 當 前 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 主 要 營 收來 源 。
Low CTE 則 已於 2025 年 底 開 始 小 量 出 貨 ， 目 前產 品 出 貨 量 已 較 2026 年初
顯 著 增 加，預 期 營 收 佔 比 將 於 2027 年 陸 續 提 升，並 於 2028 年 伴隨 近 年 陸 續
擴建 之 新 產 能 開 出 後 ，顯 著 提 高 。鑒 於 目 前 下 游 終 端 需 求 仍 以 Low Dk 產品
為主力， 研究部 預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 營 收 比 重 仍 將 高 於 Low Dk1 及 Low 
CTE 。 當 前 營 收 比 重 為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE 。 另 外 ， 台 玻 目 前 亦嘗
試 生 產 測 試 Low Dk3( 三代布 )， 後 續 若 生 產 良 率 過 關 ， 亦 將送 樣 驗 證 。 
三 、 不 急 於 產 品 漲 價 ， 堅 守 優 質 客 戶 連 結：  
台 玻 生 產 研 發 玻 纖 布數 十 年，始 終 秉 持 於 維 繫 良 好 客 戶 關 係，並 以 客 戶 需 求
為 營 運 宗 旨 。 雖 2025~2026 年以來 E 布 報 價 倍 數 增 長 ， 惟 基 於多年來與 客
戶 合作 建立 之 信任 及當前產品利潤貢獻尚屬可控範圍，故而 預期將 不急於
大 幅 反 映 產 品 報 價 ， 但 亦 不 排 除 部 分 調 整 產 品 報 價 。 
四 、 具 備 Q 布 生 產 技 術 及 能 力：  
台 玻 於 本 次 TPCA Show 上 ， 已 展 出 Q 布 玻 纖 紗 ，顯示 其 Q 布 技 術 儲 備 ， 且
已 具 備 接 單 出 貨 能 力 。 惟 觀 察 AI 伺 服 器 規 格 調 整，預期 2027 年 產 品 需 求
仍以 Low Dk 系 列 玻 纖 布 為 主 ， 大 量 搭 載 Q 布 尚 早 。 不 同 材 料 規 格 之 玻 纖
布 ， 其 製 程 方 式雖 雷 同 ， 惟 配 方 均 有 差 異 ，窯 爐 使 用 亦 不 同 。 因 此 ， 台 玻 將
視 下 游 廠 商 產 品 需 求 ， 決 議 Q 布 生 產 時 程。

2 群益投顧 
五、 研 究 部 預 估台玻 2027 年稅後 EPS 4.37 元 ：  
台玻 09/2026 營收 49.68 億 元 ，MoM+8.18% 、YoY+34.11% ，主因 Low Dk1/Low 
Dk2 等 高 階 玻 纖 布 出 貨 動 能 較 08/2026 提 升。若 以 玻 纖 布 營 收 比 重 40~45%，
且高階玻纖布佔其中 40~42% 推估， 09/2026 高階玻纖布營收貢獻約
MoM+7~9% 。 3Q26 營收 合計 約 141.41 億 元 ， QoQ+17.24% 、 YoY+31.82% ，
亦 優 於 原 預 期 之 138.80 億元， QoQ+15.08% 、 YoY+29.40% 。  
展望 2027 ~2028 年 ， 預 期 2027 年 Low Dk1/Dk2 玻 纖 布 出 貨 量 仍 將 較 2026
年 顯 著 提 升，Low CTE 營 收 貢 獻 亦 將 持 續 走 揚，2028 年 伴 隨 新 產 能 開 出 後，
有望趕上 Low Dk2 。 目 前 台 玻 Low CTE 產 品 已 陸 續 取 得 國 內 外 大 廠 認 證 ，
訂 單 能 見 度 亦 已 達 2027 年，且 2028 年 訂 單 洽 談 順 暢。因 此，供 應 鏈 產 品 需
求若 無 較 大 變 化 ，2028 年 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 仍 將持 續 成 長 。 
日本大廠 Nittobo 多 次 公 開 表 示 ， 雖 2028 年產能 倍增 ，惟 T 布 市 場 需 求 已
非 當 前 公 司 擴 產 速 度 所 能 消 化 ， 顯 示 產 品 需 求 強 勁 。 研 究 部預 估 台 玻 4Q26
底 ~2027 年 玻 纖 布 營 收 比 重 有 望 超 越 平 板 玻 璃 和 玻 璃 器 皿 。預估 2027 年營
收 649.42 億 元 ， YoY +26.21 %， 毛利率 32.01% ， 稅 後 淨 利 127.17 億 元 ，
YoY +90.06 %， 稅後 EPS 4.37 元 。  
投 資 建 議 ：  
台玻 10/20/2026 首 戰 台 灣 電 路 板 產 業 國 際 博 覽 會(TPCA Show 2026) 。本次
會 中 主 要 展 出 其 高 階 玻 纖 布。 當 前 以 Low Dk1/Dk2 為 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 主 要 營
收 來源。 Low CTE 則已於 2025 年 底 開 始 小 量 出 貨 ， 目 前 產 品 出 貨 量 已 較
2026 年 初 顯 著 增 加。 當 前 營 收 比 重 為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE 。 鑒於
Low Dk 1/Dk2 產 品 出 貨 動 能 佳 ，Low CTE 營 收 貢 獻 逐 步 提 升 下 ， 毛 利 表 現
亦 將 持 續 走 揚。產 品 供 需 結 構 尚 處 緊 缺 階 段，公 司 亦 有 望 部 分 反 映 報 價。給
予 投 資 建 議 Buy ， 目 標 價 81 元。

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Document 057: 2026年10月份美联储议息会议点评：加息暂停信息中性，等待地缘局势发展的更多信号

2026年 10月 29日

加息暂停信息中性，等待地缘局势发展的更多信号 
————2026年 10月份美联储议息会议点评 
黄恬  期货投资咨询证号：Z0021089   韩泽文 期货投资咨询号 Z0021517         
事件：美东时间 10月 28日周三，美联储公布，货币政策委员会 FOMC会议决定，将
联邦基金利率的目标区间维持在 3.75%至 4.00%不变。在 9月份决定三年来首次加息后，
此次暂停行动。虽然当前保持了耐心偏鸽，但这并不代表今年不会再加息，通过会后声明对
于经济强劲表述以及金融条件相关的表述，释放了偏鹰的信息。因此总的来看，传递的综合
信号是中性的。决议和发布会之后，会后市场的走势来看，美债短端利率回落，长端小幅回
升，美元小幅回落，黄金小幅反弹。 
点评：从会前关注来看：首先，暂停加息的政策定位。如果维持利率不变，沃什需要向
市场解释理由，并说明此举与推动 2%通胀目标回归的一致性。本次会议不更新点阵图，声
明和发布会对暂停理由的解释将更加重要；其次，美联储对就业风险的判断是否发生变化。9
月新增就业明显放缓，但失业率尚在区间波动，委员会是否将其视为需求走弱的早期信号，
将影响其愿意为压低通胀承担就业风险的程度。最后，美联储如何评估当前金融条件的实际
收紧效果。9月会议纪要指出，尽管长期美债收益率上升，股指上涨以及总体偏宽松的信贷
条件仍在支持经济增长。如果美联储维持这一判断，市场利率上行或不足以替代进一步政策
加息；如果认为融资成本已更广泛地传导至需求端，暂停加息的依据则会增强。因此，10月
即使暂停，也未必改变年内继续加息的方向，关键在于美联储认为货币政策还需要多少时间
以及多大力度，才能推动通胀持续回落。

### Page 2

- 2 -

美联储公布，货币政策委员会 FOMC会议决定，将联邦基金利率的目标区间维持在
3.75%至 4.00%不变。与 9月份 12名票委的全票赞成决议不同，本次会议 12名票委其中
有两名反对这一决议，认为应该加息。从本次会后声明来看，关于经济与政策的表达相较于
9月份议息会议做出了部分调整：首先，利率决议方面，调整为“维持目标区间在 3.75%—
4.00%”，反映本次会议暂停加息；其次，在经济问题的表述，将经济活动扩张速度从“稳
健（solid）”上调为“强劲（strong）”。有关地缘政治不确定性、国内支出韧性、生产率
增长、资本投资以及就业和失业率的表述均未改变；最后，在通胀与后续政策表述方面，美
联储继续表示通胀处于高位，但删除了 9月声明中“今天的政策行动将有助于更及时地实现
委员会 2%的目标”，改为强调“积累更多信息将帮助委员会确定适当的政策立场”，以确
保通胀及时回归 2%目标。声明仍重申，美联储将实现价格稳定。总的来看，提出暂停加息
是为了“积累更多信息”来辅助未来政策判断，则体现出了一定的“耐心”，这一点偏鸽。
但对于经济增长调整为“强劲”的表述，也体现了沃什强调近期货币政策对于经济的正向作
用，这一点偏鹰。因此整体来看属于偏中性的一个信号传递。 
从沃什的发布会内容来看：第一，关于议息会议的改革机制，美联储将从 2027年开始减
少利率制定会议的次数。八次会议虽然合适，但从 27年开始将逐步增加讨论货币政策战略的
会议次数， 减少仅聚焦于短期利率决策的会议。 同时沃什再度重申对于前瞻性指引的保留立场，
称除非在危机的特殊情况下；第二，关于 AI资本开支和经济基本面的“强劲”，他指出今年
上半年 GDP增速已从此前估计的 1.8%上修至 2.4%，第三季度增速看似进一步加快，沃什表
示近期 FOMC 讨论"主要集中在物价稳定职责上"。同时就业方面情况"相当积极"，失业率较
一年前下降，失业保险申请的四周均值处于"几代人以来最低水平"；第三，关于金融条件的限
制性问题，沃什表示他不会将当前金融条件整体定义为“紧张”，而是形容其有助于开辟通往

### Page 3

- 3 -

价格稳定的路径，对于 AI而言，他表示利率上升已经产生了“让部分资产的信用利差有所扩
大”以及“有些资产金融条件收紧”的迹象，尽管如此，他表示整体资本支出的故事"不仅强
劲，而且正在扩散"；最后，在美联储的政治独立性方面，沃什表示中期选举（定于11月 3日）
的政治因素对于本次会议决定“没有任何作用”。 
总的来看， 此次会议在暂停加息后， 从点阵图、 经济数据预测和发布会等方面的信息来看，
仍然传递了相对偏鹰派的信号：美联储强调经济的韧性，淡化加息对于供给侧冲击效果不明显
的言论，淡化目前收益率上行的冲击，并且继续强调对于通胀而言不能只看时点数据而要看趋
势，同时此次会议的表决联储委员的态度显著一致。这些信号表明，相较于加息和利率抬升带
来的负面因素而言，目前沃什仍然认为对于经济的冲击相对可控，继续坚守管理通胀这一目标
仍然是当前的首要任务。决议和发布会之后，美国 10 年期及两年期国债收益率均出现上涨，
美元指数走强，现货黄金跳水，美股由涨转跌。 
总的来看，结合着暂停加息的行动，以及声明和沃什发布会的内容，本次会议传递出相对
中性的信号：虽然此次会议暂停加息，表达了更多耐心，但是对于经济增长强劲的表述，表明
近期的利率抬升仍然没有对于经济产生负面的压力，并没有降低 12月份继续加息的预期。同
时在金融条件的表述上， 美联储虽然看到了一些收紧现象， 但是AI投资仍然处在扩张状态中，
表达出了对于这种边际上的收紧相对淡定的姿态，即金融体系对这种收紧的承受力仍然较高的
态度。决议和发布会之后，从市场的走势来看，美债短端利率回落，长端小幅回升，美元小幅
回落，黄金小幅反弹。 
展望未来 1-2个月，可以从以下几个方面把握宏观的主要矛盾的演化: 首先，地缘局势的
发展仍然在重要窗口期。进入 10月份之后，重要的地缘事件密集地汇集，俄乌谈判传来积极
信号，11 月初的谈判前也有停火，另一面则是特朗普对伊朗仍然强硬，胡塞武装和沙特的冲
突也尚未结束，因此目前的悬念仍然是 11月中上旬能否演化出俄乌和美伊的双向和平甚至停

### Page 4

- 4 -

火，这对于后续的大类资产交易的主线较为关键。从能源的角度来看，由于中东原油的出口已
经有明显恢复，“量”的压力已经有所解除，但是由于地缘溢价的因素，“价”的压力仍然较
为明显。沃什在 10月底议息会议上并没有给出更多货币政策的未来指引，但也释放出目前的
金融条件紧缩下，经济和金融市场仍然能够承受的信号，那么显然如果地缘局势仍然焦灼或者
有所升级，10 月份以来的美元偏强和美债信用利差抬升的趋势会延续，而如果地缘局势有缓
和的话，那么能源的“价”的压力缓和会压制通胀溢价，并缓和继续收紧的金融条件。目前来
看如何发展仍不明朗，需要继续跟踪； 
其次，关注中期选举结果以及结果确定后美伊战争的发展。进入 11月中旬后，APEC深
圳会议中美俄三方领导人能否会晤是后续的一个重要看点。 在这之前中期选举落地结果以及在
这之后是否仍然有美针对伊朗的军事行动较为关键。最差情景是共和党丧失两院叠加选举后美
伊仍然有军事冲突，最好情景则是共和党守住了参议院同时选举后伊核谈判落地。对于风险偏
好后续的预判应在 11月第一周根据发展相机抉择。    
最后，继续跟踪国内经济政策的发展思路，近期美联储带来的紧缩效应不仅让美国的金融
条件收紧，同时也让离岸美元流动性压力增加之下，大中华区的金融条件也在收紧。因此国内
权益市场的估值也因为这种金融条件的收缩而出现了快速的回落，但国内 Q3的 GDP增速整
体稳健。前者是金融市场的压力过度悲观地定价了基本面，后者是国内政策根据外围的不确定
性目前也保持一定的定力。因此，后续如果外围局势好转，那么如果有国内金融条件修复的时
间窗口，这种悲观预期将会矫正。反之则是金融市场的压力会逐步体现在 Q4基本面上。 
大类资产来看，从 9月份议息会议到此次会议前，基础格局是美元强势，同时美债利率强
势后引发全球的信用收缩，目前来看，10 月底的议息会议释放了中性的信号，近期外围市场
也有金融条件冲击小幅缓和的信号。后续来看，地缘局势发展是判断这种强美元和信用收缩压
力继续深化还是缓和的关键。后续无非是两条演化路径：

### Page 5

- 5 -

目前提防的仍然是能源压力缓解难，美债紧缩压力向股市和经济的进一步传导；伺机交易
的是能源压力缓和之下，信用紧缩和衰退压力的缓解以及美元流动性压力的缓和，给国内政策
带来的空间以及权益市场带来的估值修复。 
当前权益和商品指数的情绪都在较低位置，整体来看继续做空的性价比在大幅下降，但驱
动是否反转仍需更多信号。国内权益市场当前阶段，有“政策底”的预期，但仍然需要“政策
底”的标志性事件，而“市场底”的驱动仍然需要外部局势不确定性的落地，目前来看，进一
步下探的空间或有限，但也不能忽视尾部风险。从时间上来看，11 月上旬还是下旬之后才能
确认反弹成立尚待观察，配置上先以消费和金融等为主导，等待科技的下跌动能进一步消耗。
由于 11月中下旬中美俄元首会晤值得交易，因此可以通过牛市价差或者买虚值看涨的模式进
行参与。 
对于商品市场而言， 仍然处在多能化空国内需求侧商品的阶段， 不过能化目前整体在高位，
赔率有所下降，是否能够继续空中加油也需要根据地缘局势的演化而定。而9月下旬以后，很
多内需型产能过剩品种也已经有了一轮流畅的大幅下跌， 目前也在受到成本或者供给收缩的支
撑。后续来看，如果朝着地缘局势难缓解金融条件继续收缩的方向发展，那近期强势抗跌品种
可能出现补跌；而如果地缘局势向积极方向转变伴随金融条件的修复，那么金融属性强的有色
品种将出现反弹，并可能逐步伴随内需型商品的筑底。总体来看短期目前应聚焦于截面策略以
强弱对冲攻守相济为主，方向策略等待更为明确的指引。

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026 年 10 月30 日

1/3 
2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測（1 次速報） 
～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅ながら４四半期連続のプラス成長を予想、景気回復基調は維持 
11 月16 日に公表される2026 年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋
0.1％）と予測する。４四半期連続のプラス成長になるだろう1。 
物価高による下押しや耐久財需要の反動に加え、天候不順の影響もあって個人消費が減少する
一方、設備投資が高い伸びになることが下支えになる見込みである。設備投資の伸びには特殊要
因も一部含まれているが、それを除いても堅調だったと考えられる。個人消費が弱含むなかでも
プラス成長を維持するとみられることは、企業部門の底堅さが景気を下支えしていることを示し
ている。過去３四半期に比較的高い成長が続いた後であることも踏まえれば、今回の減速をもっ
て景気の基調が弱まったと判断する必要はないだろう。家計部門の弱さは懸念材料だが、企業部
門の底堅さは維持されており、景気は引き続き緩やかな回復基調にあると評価できる。

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
７-９月期のＧＤＰを評価する際には、原油輸入と公的在庫の動きをセットで捉える必要があ
る。４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を
通じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油
が放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt
（前期比年率寄与度▲2.1％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げ
は、公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じるとみられる。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって
原油輸入が回復するため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因になる。一方、輸入の回復に
伴って国家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小す
るとみられる。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.4％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋1.5％
Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し上げることが予想される。

1 10 月 30 日時点で入手可能な経済指標を元に作成している。国際収支や家計調査等、今後公表される経済指標の結果
を踏まえて予測値を修正する可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/3 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げと公的在庫変動による押し上げは、ともに原油の供給環境が改
善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一
体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約するリスクが低下していることである。
ＧＤＰ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用
可能な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むし
ろ前向きに捉えるべきだろう。

需要項目別の動向 
個人消費は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減少を予想する。８～９月に各地で相次いだ大雨や台風などの
天候不順が外出機会やサービス消費を抑制した可能性がある。また、４-６月期には自動車やエア
コンなどの耐久財販売が増加したが、７-９月期にはこうした一時的な押し上げ効果が剥落した。
加えて、物価高による節約志向が根強いなか、食料品や日用品などの非耐久財消費にも弱さがみ
られる。高い賃上げや底堅い雇用環境は下支え要因となっているものの、天候不順という一時的
な要因もあって個人消費は総じて力強さを欠く結果になったとみられる。 
設備投資は前期比＋2.0％と、高い伸びを予想する。４-６月期には特許権の海外譲渡に伴う研
究開発投資の減少という特殊要因があったが、７-９月期はその影響が剥落することが押し上げ要
因になった面はある。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を除いても設備投資は明確に増加したと考え
られ、底堅い動きとなっている。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応した省力化
投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資への需要は根強く、企業の設備投資意欲は引き続き強い。設備投
資計画も高い伸びとなっており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断してよ
いだろう。 
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れとなる見
込みである。両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。 
輸出は前期比＋1.2％と４四半期連続の増加を予想する。４-６月期に大口要因によって研究開
発サービスの受取が急増した分が剥落することで、サービス輸出が減少する一方、財輸出が高い
伸びとなり、財貨・サービス輸出全体では増加を維持したとみられる。半導体製造装置などの資
本財輸出が好調だった。地域別では、ＥＵ向けが低調だった一方、ＡＳＥＡＮ、ＮＩＥｓ向けの
資本財輸出が伸びている。また、４-６月期に急減した中東向け輸出が持ち直したことも押し上げ
につながっている。 
輸入は前期比＋3.3％と大幅な増加が予想される。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が
大幅に減少していたが、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄
与度は前期比▲0.3％Pt（前期比年率▲1.3％Pt）になったと予想される。 
ただし、この外需寄与度のマイナスは、資源輸入の回復に伴う輸入の増加によって生じるもの

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
である。その裏側では公的在庫変動が前期比＋0.4％ポイント、前期比年率では＋1.5％ポイント
の大幅プラス寄与になると見込まれる。両者は併せて評価する必要がある。 
民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.1％Ptと予想する。製品在庫はプラスが予想される一方、流通
在庫の下押しが大きくなるとみられる。 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.9％と、２四半期連続の減少を予想する。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因
となっており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。また、公共投資も同▲1.5％と減少を
予想するほか、政府消費についても、４-６月期の高い伸びの反動から前期比▲0.3％と減少を見
込む。

AVC (3017.TW): Liquid cooling rising penetration and AI servers ramp-up 
to drive growth ahead; Buy
19 October 2026 | 3:24AM HKT
3-month revenues preview: We expect AVC’s monthly revenues to continue 
sequential growth through Dec 2026, leading 4Q26 revenues to grow at +42% YoY/ 
+15% QoQ, driven by: (1) new AI server racks ramp-up, driving the overall liquid 
cooling penetration rate. As we highlighted in Oct (report link), we expect AI server 
rack (NVL72-equivalent) shipment (incl. Nvidia and AMD) to reach 63k/ 110k/ 164k 
in 2026-28E, reflecting our positive view on AI server racks. (2) Its major ASIC AI 
server customer’s new model ramp-up, driving its air-cooling and liquid-cooling 
components, along with a rising liquid cooling penetration rate. (3) Its customer base 
expansion to a new ASIC AI server, to further drive its liquid cooling components in 
4Q26. And (4) increasing adoption rate of ultra-thin VC in consumer electronics to fit 
the lightweight design and major customer’s foldable phone model. We expect AVC’s 
net income to grow at a +39% CAGR in 2026-28E, riding on the strong AI spending, a 
growing liquid cooling penetration rate, and AVC’s leading market position in total 
solutions (air cooling, liquid cooling with various components / systems). Maintain 
Buy.

### Page 2

Earnings revisions: We factor in AVC’s Sep revenue and revise down our 2026E net 
income by -1%, mainly on lower revenues in 4Q26E, reflecting the slow consumer 
electronics under high memory costs and AI servers model transition. We revise up our 
2027-28E net income by 4% / 0%, mainly on higher revenues and GMs. Our 2027-28E 
revenue is revised up by 1% / 1%, mainly on a higher contribution from AI server liquid 
cooling components. 2027-28E GM are revised up to reflect the rising liquid cooling 
components driving profitability.

Valuation: We continue to use a near-term P/E to derive our 12-month target price and 
update our target P/E multiple to 30.6x 2027E EPS (vs. 30.3x previously), which is still 
derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple correlation. Our target P/E 
multiple is above AVC’s historical average +1stv. forward trading P/E, reflecting our 
positive view on AVC riding on the rising penetration rate of liquid-cooled AI servers. 
With our updated target P/E and earnings estimates, we raise our 12-month target price 
to NT$4,736 (from NT$4,513). Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - AVC 
We are Buy-rated on AVC. Our 12-month target price for AVC is based on a near-term 
P/E, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our target price of 
NT$4,736 on a target P/E multiple of 30.6x applied to our forward-year EPS (2027E). 
Our target P/E is derived from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers 
in technology hardware.  
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected adoption of liquid cooling; (2) 
lower-than-expected demand for AI servers; (3) fiercer-than-expected competition; 
and (4) a slower-than-expected recovery in general servers.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

MediaTek  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 results preview – all eyes
on AI ASIC  development
MediaTek remains competitive in the AI ASIC service industry
given strong execution and design capability. We look forward to
3Q26 updates.
We expect US$2bn revenue of AI ASIC in 4Q26: MediaTek will host its 3Q26
earnings call on 10/30 Friday. It posted 10% Q/Q growth in preliminary 3Q26
revenue. Into 4Q26, given weak smartphone and consumer (eg. TV) demand, we

expect non-AI revenue to decline 10%-20% Q/Q. That said, thanks to US$2bn of TPU
v8t (ZebraFish) mass production, we expect 4Q26 revenue to grow 30%-40% Q/Q.
For gross margin, we expect 4Q26 to decline to 44-45%, as AI ASIC will likely dilute
the corporate margin.  Please see Exhibit 1   for our earnings preview.
Key focus for the call – all about AI ASIC (see next section for 2028 AI ASIC):
1. 2027 AI ASIC TAM and MediaTek’s market share: We expect MediaTek to

modestly raise its 2027 AI ASIC TAM or market share outlook, given better visibility
on TPU v8t (ZebraFish) CoWoS and substrate supply. Our  Asia supply chain checks
suggest 180k of CoWoS capacity could be allocated to TPU v8t, supporting
production of  3.6mn units, of which we assume 3mn units will be recognized as
MediaTek revenue in 2027. If substrate availability improves, we believe TSMC’s
CoWoS allocation to the TPU v8t could increase to 200k-220k units.

2. Google TPU v9 status (covered by Brian Nowak): This project is MediaTek's major
2028 revenue driver, and where investors appeared concerned, given uncertainty
around Intel (covered by Joe Moore) EMIB-T substrate supply. We expect Mediatek
to share that the TPU v9 (HumuFish) project is roughly on schedule for early 2027
tape-out, with high-volume production starting in early 2028, as previously guided.
We previously discussed the possibility of MediaTek using TSMC's CoWoS to help

with any 2028 TPU production concerns, and TSMC management indicated they
would be willing to help customers to smooth production if they were struggling
with EMIB-T supply. If MediaTek did want to tape out another CoWoS packaging
version of TPU v9, we believe it could take around a year based on existing norms.
Whether MediaTek adopts CoWoS for TPU v9, or waits for the next generation
(IceFish, or TPU v10) as a back-up, it currently is only using EMIB-T.

3. Google TPU v10 competition: As we described in Google ASIC, who gets what
for design services?, we see GUC and Alchip as partners for MediaTek coordinating
around the TPU v10 IceFish design service.
4. Second customer win? In our Nvidia partnership note, we saw a higher chance of
MediaTek winning a second or even third CSP customer (MediaTek had indicated it
was engaging with some customers, Aug. 31 interview). MediaTek may also consider
China CSP projects if its 3nm wafer allocation is sufficient for 2027-28, and projects

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey. Voting
opens 10 November 2026.

1. Substrate supply is the key, not demand, for MediaTek
production for Google TPUs
TSMC CoWoS could   support 2nm (Humufish) MediaTek's production for TPUs in 2028:

In our earlier report, MediaTek: 2Q26 results preview: Smartphone weak, but Cloud AI
taking off; OW (26 Jul 2026), we noted that, in our view,  MediaTek might secure a portion
of CoWoS capacity for 2nm TPUs to ensure minimum volume production. However, we
believe, in-line with MediaTek's intention, it will use EMIB-T from Intel  as the primary
solution to reduce costs and scale volume in 2028, if execution proceeds well.
During TSMC's earlier earnings call, TSMC management shared that it was willing to help

customers that may be struggling with Intel EMIB-T supply to smooth production, which
suggested to us that it would be willing to offer support even if CoWoS were the back-up/
second source. However, our recent industry checks suggest that MediaTek may not need
to adopt TSMC CoWoS for its TPU v9 as the EMIB-T substrate (for v9) output is tracking
its plan. If MediaTek did decide to use CoWoS, we believe the tape-out of the CoWoS
version of TPU v9 could take a year, based on previous norms.

CoWoS constraints eased, but ABF substrate supply still the gating factor for TPU
production bull case: In our recent AI supply chain tracker, we noted that MediaTek’s
180k CoWoS-S bookings imply ~3.6 units of TPU 8t (ZebraFish) in 2027, above our original

### Page 5

3mn shipment assumption. However, we continue to bake in some conservatism as we had
assumed ABF substrate shortages. So,  we still forecast 3mn units in 2027.

2. Could MediaTek add second and third AI ASIC customers?
Through the NVLink Fusion platform, MediaTek's XPU customers can leverage NVIDIA's
(covered by Joe Moore) scale-up and scale-out networking technologies to build AI server

systems. Our Asia supply chain checks suggest MediaTek may target subsystem builds for
a second CSP customer rather than supplying the XPU itself. To account for the impact of
the NVLink Fusion platform, we now assume an initial revenue contribution in 2028,
based on approximately 100k subsystem units at an ASP of US$25k, which is a rough
estimate based on materials needed, including HBM.
We therefore assume a US$2.5bn revenue contribution in 2028, based on 100k units and

an ASP of US$25k, including HBM chip consignment, known-good dies (KGDs) for
compute chips, and chiplets.
On margin trends, management highlighted continued value creation for customers across
both silicon and packaging design. As a result, we expect ASICs to remain accretive to
operating margins. At the gross margin level, management indicated that margins vary by
project, potentially reflecting support for full-rack systems. We model 2nm TPU gross

margins for MediaTek at c.35% (vs. c.40% for 3nm TPUs) given the larger revenue scale.
We continue to expect operating margins of ~20–25%, above the smartphone business
unit's 15–20%.

3. Why could TSMC be motivated  to support MediaTek and
Google?
TSMC's CoWoS-L solution carries a structurally higher cost vs. EMIB due to its larger
reticle size, as we discussed in The Foundry Floorplan: TSMC preview update; CoWoS vs.
EMIB competition (14 Apr 2026). We think TSMC may want to address the growing
competition from Intel's EMIB-T, for which we estimate 40-45k wafer equivalent capacity

in 2028 based on our Asia supply chain checks, implying a 10-15% share of the 2.5D
packaging market.
For MediaTek to adopt TSMC's CoWoS-L as a second source alongside EMIB-T, the RDL
(redistribution layer) connection to the interposer requires tape-out of three additional
photomask layers. However, our Asia channel checks suggest that Google may be willing
to absorb the incremental costs associated with dual sourcing, which could allow
MediaTek to sustain its gross margin.

We think MediaTek may produce 3mn units for TPUv9 shipments in
2028 with support from Intel's EMIB-T
Based on Asia supply chain checks, average EMIB-T capacity is around 5kwpm in 2026,
which we expect to further scale to 15-20kwpm in 2027 and 40-45kwpm in 2028. All of
the capacity mentioned above is converted for 12-inch wafer equivalent for customers'

easier understanding compared  with TSMC's CoWoS capacity.
Although some small projects are ongoing, we still see MediaTek/Google Humufish
consuming most of the capacities during 2027-28. We now expect MediaTek to produce
3mn units of TPUv9 (Humufish) with support from Intel's EMIB-T from late 2027-2028.
We see the current average EMIB-M capacity at 110kwpm, based on supply chain checks,
which could slightly decrease to 95kwpm in 2027 as Intel is converting some capacities

into EMIB-T.

Price target remains at NT$6,188
We continue to derive our price target (our base-case scenario value) from a residual
income model. All of  our key valuation assumptions remain unchanged: cost of equity at
9.2% (beta of 1.2, risk-free rate of 2%, and risk premium of 6%), intermediate growth rate
of 12%, and terminal growth rate of 3.0%.
Our bull- and bear-case scenario values also remain at NT$8,500 and  NT$3,150,
respectively.

Relative valuation
We view the P/E as relatively attractive – even after the share price rally – given strong AI
demand. Although MediaTek faces smartphone headwinds in 2026, we believe it is time to
look beyond 2026. We think this margin pressure is fully priced in, while TPU upside is
not. We believe the stock should trade more than +1SD above its historical average,
supported by strong TPU demand. Our  price target of NT$6,188 implies 42x  2027 EPS, on

our estimates.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
MediaTek (2454.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: cost of equity 9.2%, intermediate growth
rate 12.0%, terminal growth rate 3.0%.
Risks to Upside
n Edge AI proliferates smartphone replacement cycle.
n Smartphone demand rises in China and other EMs.
n New products attract high demand, resulting in market share gains.
n Google's TPU spurs demand.
Risks to Downside

n Smartphone demand deteriorates in China and other EMs.
n Competition heats up, resulting in pricing competition.
n New products attract low demand, causing market share loss.
n Margin dilution is more severe.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 500,000원 
현재주가 (10.6) 272,000원 
상승여력 83.8%

3Q26 Review: 악재의 중첩, 
4분기까지 지켜보자 
최정환 CFA

jeonghwan.choi@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 1,640,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
739,000 
방위산업

◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
◼ 컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
◼ 4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
4Q26E-2Q27E 지상방산 및 항공우주 EBITDA에 Target 12MF EV/EBITDA 25.0x 
적용, 상장 자회사 가치 SOTP 통해 목표주가 산출. 3Q26 실적 아쉬우나 투자포
인트에는 변함 없어 멀티플은 변경 없이 적용. 27년 실적 추정치 상향에 따라 본
업 가치 상승, 26년 스페인 자주포 양산 및 미국 자주포 시제 사업자로 선정되며 
현지화 전략이 유효했음을 증명. 3Q26 실적에 대한 우려를 소화하며 글로벌 피어
들과의 멀티플 격차 축소를 전망함

컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 6.1조원(-6.0% YoY), 영업이익 4,520억원(-46.2% YoY, 
OPM 7.4%)으로 컨센서스인 매출액 7.4조원 및 영업이익 1.1조원을 크게 하회
하는 실적 시현. 하회의 주요 요인으로는 3Q26 필리조선소 적자폭 확대, 지상
방산 부문 실적이 부진한 영향. 지상방산 부문의 경우 매출액 1.2조원(-41.0% 
YoY), 영업이익 220억원(-96.2% YoY, OPM 1.7%)을 시현했는데, 폴란드 관련 
매출이 부재했던 1Q26 영업이익률 16.9%보다도 낮은 실적을 보임. 이는 국내
외 사업 모두에서 악재가 겹쳐 발생했던 것으로 보이는데 국내 사업에서는 마
진율이 낮은 사업이 매출로 인식되었고 수출 사업에서는 폴란드 매출 부재 뿐 
아니라 이집트, 호주 K9 자주포 매출 인식 또한 예상 대비 부진해 고정비 레버
리지 효과로 3Q26 영업이익률이 크게 훼손된 것으로 추정. 이외 대전사업장 복
구에 따른 비용, 기타 사업 충당금 등 총 500억원 수준의 일회성 비용이 발생했
으며 영업외손익에서는 원화 강세에 따른 환손실이 주로 반영되었음

4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향 
1Q26에서 3Q26까지 이뤄지지 않았던 폴란드 EC2 K9 자주포 30문 인도는 
4Q26에 집중될 것으로 보이며 폴란드 EC1 천무 또한 기존 가이던스였던 26년 
천무 40대에서 60대로 인도 대수 상향이 이뤄짐. 이에 따라 4Q26 지상방산 영
업이익률은 22.8%로 회복을 전망. 27년에는 폴란드 K9 자주포 및 천무 인도 대
수 증가로 26년 수출 비중 58.0%에서 77.2%로 증가, 이에 따라 영업이익률 또
한 매출 믹스 개선에 따라 19.8%에서 28.0%로 개선을 전망

- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상 
- Underperform(시장수익률 하회) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450/KS) 
일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입 
2026-10-30

조선/방산. 한승한, RA. 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 1,300,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 760,000 원 
상승여력: 71.1%

Signal: 3Q26, 수출 물량 저조 및 국내 낮은 마진 물량 인식으로 어닝 쇼크 
Key: 4Q부터 내년까지 수출 물량 빠르게 회복하며 실적 성장 이어갈 전망 
Step: 연내 새로운 중장기 성장동력이 될 중동 수주계약 기대. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 수출 물량 납품 저조로 어닝 쇼크  
3Q26 연결 매출액 6 조 943 억원(-6.0% YoY, -34.4% QoQ), 영업이익 4,520억원
(-46.2% YoY, -66.9% QoQ)으로, 시장예상치(1조 1,615억원)를 대폭 하회하는 어닝 쇼
크 기록했다. ①폴란드 중심의 수출 물량의 매출 인식 시점이 4 분기로 이연되며, 수출 물량 
비중이 감소한 점이 컸다. ②내수 물량 또한 감소했으며, 특히 국내 물량 중에서도 상대적
으로 마진율 낮은 유무인 무기체계 초도 물량이 이번 분기에 집중됐다. ③내수&수출 전반
적인 지상방산 매출 비중 감소에 따른 고정비 부담이 컸으며, ④일회성 요인으로 대전 사업
장 사고에 따른 복구 비용 약 300 억원과 충당금 및 기타 비용 총 160 억원, 그리고 환율 
하락으로 영업이익의 약 10% 수준의 감소 효과 발생했다. 다만 4Q26 부터 이번 분기 이
연된 폴란드 중심의 수출 물량 인식이 반영되면서 큰 폭의 탑라인 및 이익 성장 시현할 전
망이며, 대전 사업장 또한 내년 상반기 가동 재개 예상됨에 따라 지상방산 부문은 전년 대비 
성장 폭 두드러지며 사측 가이던스 15% 매출 성장 충분히 달성 가능할 전망이다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 130 만원으로 하향 
한화에어로스페이스에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가 기존 160 만원에서 130
만원으로 하향한다. 최근 Peer 그룹의 주가 하락에 따라 멀티플 하향 조정했기 때문이다. 
폴란드 천무 유도탄 JV에 이어 현지화가 전제된 K9 3차 계약을 추진 중이며, 국내 장약 생
산능력 증설을 통한 유럽향 수요 대응과 미국 내 생산거점 구축까지 진행 중에 있다. 또한 
미국 차륜형 자주포 사업 시제 선정에 이은 궤도형 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다. 중동 국
가들의 방공무기체계에 대한 계약 논의는 매우 가시적인 상황이며, 사우디 MNG 사업 수주 
또한 시점의 문제일 뿐이다. 기존 유럽에 더해 중동은 현지화 전략을 기반으로 동사의 새로
운 중장기 성장동력이 될 지역이며, 올해 4 분기부터 해당 수주 모멘텀과 함께 큰 폭의 실적 
성장이 맞물리며 리레이팅 가능할 전망이다. 선제적 매수 기회라는 판단이다.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
We reaffirm our expectation  that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board will again increase the cash rate at the November
meeting. Today’s inflation data and Senate testimony are
the final pieces of information before the RBA Monetary
Policy Board (MPB) meets next week. Recent data and

communication have consistently pointed to the staff
recommending an increase in the cash rate. We expect a
majority of the external members to agree, though some
members might dissent.
Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions. It

will therefore deem that further policy tightening is needed.
The RBA has made it clear that it is down-weighting the recent
easing in the labour market, even though this was more than it
forecast. In today’s opening statement  to the Senate hearing,
the Governor said, “Despite the unemployment rate recently
rising a little more than we had expected, the labour market
seems a little tighter than we had previously judged. The
evidence for this is elevated underlying inflationary pressures.

That is, when we exclude some of the large volatile price
movements such as fuel we see inflationary pressure in areas
that reflect domestic demand for goods and services.” In the
Senate, the Governor also said that they were not worried
about the current pace of increase in unemployment.
Of course, this takes no account of the lagged relationship
between the labour market and inflation. The argument is that
if that measure of inflation last quarter was too high, it must

therefore be that the labour market is too tight. Nor does it
allow sufficiently for non-labour cost shocks, which business
surveys indicate are the real issue. But whether this judgement
by the RBA turns out to be right or not will not be revealed
for some time, certainly not soon enough to change its mind
before next week. And while some MPB members might have
a more optimistic view about labour supply trends than the
RBA staff, they are likely to be a minority.

The RBA is also relatively sanguine about the current
downturn in the housing market. The Governor and other
RBA officials are at pains to highlight that they do not target
housing prices; nor should they. The RBA considers that most,
though not all, of the current downswing is an expected effect
of tighter monetary policy.
RBA to hike in November, as inflation remains too high
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The question then turns to the future beyond November. While

we still expect a hawkish tone in the RBA’s communication
until inflation has clearly turned down, further hikes beyond
next week are not currently our base case. Clearly, though, the
risks of another hike – most likely in February – have increased.
Our current forecast for Q4 inflation would seem to give them
cover for such a move. We note, however, that home-building
costs in the CPI have undershot expectations in the past couple
of months. This might imply a different view of the extent of

domestic demand pressures than the RBA currently believes.
However, a lot can happen in three months, as we have just
seen. The conflict in the Middle East  could escalate further,
lifting energy prices beyond our current base case. It could
also go the other way, though, if the US and Iran manage to
strike a post-midterm-election deal. And as their counterparts
in New Zealand highlighted today, one risk is that the global
economy weakens suddenly. In the post-meeting statement ,

the RBNZ Monetary Policy Committee said, “the world
economy could tip abruptly into a downturn causing weaker
inflation over the medium term.” This risk was something the
RBNZ MPC thought could crystallise in the next six weeks.
Whether on this timetable or something more drawn out, this
possibility does not appear to be on the RBA’s radar. Nor is it
our base case, though we have previously worried that the AI
financing boom could hit a wall quite suddenly.

“We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA
Monetary Policy Board will again increase the
cash rate at the November meeting"

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related
risks through 2027; although, in the absence of another shock,
we expect them to remain on hold.
The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view

on the breadth of growth, the 3.4% annualised gain for GDP
benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household
consumption, 12.7% growth in non-residential investment
as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment
was effectively offset by net exports’ drag given most of
the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is
imported. This is not to say that US firms won’t benefit from

the investment once complete, but simply that much of the
initial return goes to the foreign businesses who provide the
necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks
remain for the consumer, the increase in nominal spending
seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis,
consumption flat in the month following a 0.5% gain in
August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the
outlook is expected to see household consumption growth

average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE
inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr medium-term target as
the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline
and core measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy
rates unchanged in October. Of note from the meeting
communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks

for inflation, but also downside risks for growth. "While the
full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying
inflation has remained broadly stable so far and wages do not
show a material response". "Financing conditions have [also]
tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth".
In the press conference, President Lagarde noted that,
between the October and December meetings, a significant
amount of new economic information will be released,

allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.
Based on the October meeting communications, we continue
to expect a rate hike at the December meeting but believe this
will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the
spectrum of risks Europe faces is broad and skewed towards
inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that
she will step down as ECB President in February ahead of the

scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI .
Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in the month, largely
owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in
domestic and international holiday travel & accommodation.
The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than
anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).
While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation –
housing in particular looks to be cooling faster than expected

as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are
still showing persistence. Highlighting this is market services
ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator
of price pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this
metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail points
to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for
another chunky read in Q4.
With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts,

and some of the energy risks flagged then materialising, we
continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp
rate hike to 4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate
hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market
expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding instead to wait for
further information before taking the next step with policy. The
decision was made 4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A

25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening in 2027
(Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems
probable; however, each decision will depend on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady  at its October
meeting. Based on prior comments by key members, the
decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook

and Cleveland Fed President Hammack’s dissents in favour
of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at
a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Chair
Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic
growth outside of tech-related investment (evinced by Q3
GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in
the statement. However, inflation “remains elevated” and the

FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable
return of annual inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will
help the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests
another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter, the FOMC
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
A lesson to be learned
The RBNZ delivered the biggest shock to markets outside of
a crisis period in over 20 years this week by leaving the OCR
unchanged. Markets had priced around a 98% probability of
a 25bp increase, and all major domestic analysts concurred.

After the CPI data the week earlier, we belatedly shifted our
call to a hike despite plenty of good arguments in favour of
the RBNZ sticking to its plan to pause. With the benefit of
hindsight, too much weight was put on market pricing and
short-term inflation drivers and not enough on the medium
term. That’s an important lesson to be learned.
The highlight of an action-packed week of central banking news
was the unexpected decision of the RBNZ to leave the OCR at

2.75%, despite near unanimous analyst expectations and market
pricing in favour of a 25bp hike. We had shifted sides from a
hold to a hike just a week earlier, in the wake of a CPI outcome
that was weaker than RBNZ expectations on the surface, but
with some evidence of firm core inflation undertones.
While we clearly cannot claim to have gotten this call right,
we always thought that the outcome was not the “done deal”
that markets had priced in. For example, in our preview of the

meeting we noted:
“We still think a more measured tightening approach is
appropriate from here. Raising the OCR at every meeting
until something ‘breaks’ could be a risky strategy if downside
growth concerns crystalise.”
And we saw a vote on the decision as very possible:
“A vote seems very possible as differences in view will likely
remain among the MPC about how much the medium-term
inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.”
The 4:3 vote that was ultimately required to bring the MPC

to a decision is a testament to just how finely balanced the
decision was. We had thought that the hike was perhaps
around a 60-70% probability whereas the Record of Meeting
implies something more like a 50:50 call.
In the final analysis, the RBNZ has merely followed through on
the pause it signalled for this meeting back in the September
Monetary Policy Statement. All MPC members continue to see
a case for a higher OCR in time, but there are different views

around the timing of those increases. Governor Breman noted
that further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations. Indeed, the Governor noted
in later comments to press that a December hike was a “high”
probability and that the outlook remains for OCR “hikes” as the
base case. This suggests that the RBNZ’s view on the terminal
OCR hasn’t really shifted down from the 3.25% level presented

back in September. And it may be reasonable to speculate,
subject to data evolving as the RBNZ expects, that a modest
upgrade in those forward OCR forecasts could be in the offing.
So how did we all get this so wrong? This outcome was a real
shock given the historical record shows that over the history of
the OCR the market has without exception correctly predicted
a 25bp hike when the implied probability built into markets
was as high as it was. Clearly some recalibration of the model

is required. We can identify two key areas where lessons
should be learned: too much weight on market pricing; and not
enough on drivers of medium-term inflation.
Market pricing did not adequately reflect the
MPC’s desire to shift strategy .
Firstly, and most importantly, we put too much weight on
market pricing and too little on the RBNZ’s clearly stated
strategy of preferring a pause at the October meeting to
assess the impact of interest rate increases already done. In our

Preview we incorrectly surmised that silence equated to assent
and that market pricing of a hike would make an OCR hike the
less disruptive option – especially given the sense that a hike
to 3% was still likely in December as implied by the RBNZ’s
September forecasts. From a macroeconomic perspective,
raising rates six weeks early is of little consequence.
This logic, as sound as it was, was wrong, as market pricing did
not reflect a large enough change in the medium-term inflation

outlook to justify a change in RBNZ strategy. Markets at least
partially held the view, based on a long history of revealed
RBNZ preference, that the RBNZ keeps moving the OCR in the
same direction until confronted with compelling evidence that
a halt to the cycle is in prospect. That operating approach may
be under review now.
It’s not uncommon for other central banks to take breaks
while amid an interest rate cycle to gather information on
the impact of work already done before moving forward.

This week’s pause by the FOMC in the face of a fierce set of
monthly inflation readings in the last month is an example.
Pausing does not need to imply stopping or reversing. Indeed,
taking a more measured approach could prove to be solid
risk management – as we identified in our MPR preview note.
Risk management concerns probably should have greater
prominence in this cycle given we are seeing a measure of
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
supply side cost shocks that might not be persistent. The
Statement of Record contains a wealth of material related to
these sorts of uncertainties and concerns. Markets were not
listening to these concerns, however.
An insufficient focus on drivers of medium-term

inflation
Markets were not listening to the alarm bells because there
was a heavy focus on factors that may be more relevant to the
short-term inflation outlook than medium-term inflation. Top
of mind were the rise in energy prices and fall in the exchange
rate. Both factors look set to significantly boost the short-
term inflation outlook – for example we currently forecast
December quarter annual inflation of 4.3%, with upside risks
from there if fuel prices do not fall over the balance of the

quarter. However, our analysis of the data surprises suggested
no significant shift to the implied RBNZ OCR track one year
ahead. Upward pressure on the future OCR projection from
the lower exchange rate and stronger GDP was balanced
by higher long term interest rates and the lower Q3 CPI
outcome. Higher energy prices were not seen as a key factor
as they don’t impact medium term inflation unless inflation
expectations are dislodged (no evidence was found of that

since September) or if the increase in energy prices is viewed
as persistent (futures markets continue to imply lower forward
energy prices, although MPC hawks clearly see risks of
persistent inflation coming from supply shocks).
The RBNZ’s communications at the MPR and in its aftermath
are directing markets to pay more attention to medium-term
inflation drivers in the future. Hence the references to the
strength of demand and inflation expectations. The risk to

the RBNZ’s inflation forecasts are to the upside, and hence
we might reasonably expect some upgrade of the OCR profile
when we see new forecasts in December. But it’s not a done
deal by any means as data and events between now and then
will dictate what stance the RBNZ takes. This week’s business
and consumer confidence reports do not seem to present many
smoking guns of imminent second-round inflation pressures,
even though business and consumer sentiment seems to be

taking the higher oil price in its stride.
Is core inflation high enough yet to shift the
strategy?
Another interesting point made by the Governor was that the
markets (and analysts) misinterpreted how the September
quarter CPI was read by the RBNZ, which evidently took
greater comfort from the outcome. Governor Breman has noted
she wished the RBNZ has protocols in place to communicate
with markets the RBNZ’s view on that CPI data given that it
seems to have been a factor that figured importantly in at least

some MPC member’s thinking. Headline inflation was lower
than the RBNZ expected and importantly the core inflation
measures, that we had thought might have been of concern to
the MPC, do not yet seem to have been at a level sufficient to
shift the RBNZ from its pause-then-hike strategy. We remain
sceptical that a 3.25% OCR will prove sufficient to bring core
inflation down significantly. But core inflation appears to need
to lift for the MPC to become more agitated. It likely will lift,

but we won’t get more data on that until January 2027.
December hike likely but not a done deal
We expect a further 25bp hike at this year’s final meeting on 9
December and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases at the
June and September Monetary Policy Statement meetings.
Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from September 2027 has
remained unchanged throughout, as has our assessment of a
3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes without
saying that the evolution of monetary policy will depend on
how both global events and key data evolve. Therefore, as the
RBNZ noted itself, the exact timing of the tightening profile
is highly uncertain and even the tightening we forecast at the
December meeting should not be regarded as a done deal.
Looking ahead, the ‘hardest’ data to come will be the Q3
labour market surveys where we anticipate a small fall in

the unemployment rate to 5.5% but still moderate wage
pressures. The December quarter RBNZ inflation expectations
surveys will be of significant interest (17, 20 and 24
November) for MPC members looking for persistent inflation
impacts. The usual run of monthly activity and selected prices
indicators will be useful in narrowing down Q4 growth and
inflation momentum. And finally, the MPC’s focus on external
risks will imply keeping an eye on global fixed income, equity

and credit markets for signs of imminent calamity.
Finally, November 7 is the general election. A close race is
envisaged that could impact on the RBNZ’s mandate. We
will be offering client briefings on Monday 9 November and
Tuesday 10 November to outline the immediate implications
of the election outcome for markets and the economy. Contact
your relationship manager for details.

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Nov 3, Last: 4.60, Westpac f/c: 4.85
Market f/c: 4.85, Range: 4.60 to 4.85
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) will decide to raise the cash rate by 25bps to
4.85% at the November meeting.

Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions.
Recent data and communication have consistently pointed to
the RBA staff recommending an increase in the cash rate. We
expect a majority of the external board members to agree,

though some members might dissent.
AUS: Oct Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –1.1, Westpac f/c: –1.3
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
1.1% in Sep with downward revisions shaving a further 0.4%
off prices. The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2%
from their March peak, annual growth stalling flat. Cotality
reported 97% of capital city suburbs experienced falls over the
three months to September.
The October update is set to be another weak one with an

added hit coming from the RBA’s interest rate rise in late
September. The daily measure (covering the five major capital
cities rather than all of Australia) is tracking a 1.5% decline vs
1.3% in September. The detail points to slightly bigger falls
in Sydney, Brisbane and Perth with Melbourne continuing
to record more moderate declines. Note that seasonality is
usually a slight positive in the October month.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –6.1, Westpac f/c: –7.0

Market f/c: 2.0, Range: –7.0 to 5.0
Dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the annual
pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. Weakness centred on unit
approvals with private detached house approvals actually
posting a solid rebound from a July month fall, touching a new
cycle high (up 18.4%yr).
We expect the September update to show a much clearer
weakening. New construction is coming under pressure
from multiple fronts: interest rate rises; a sharp downturn in

established markets; and rising costs – with a great deal of
uncertainty around each of these. New home sales have fell
sharply through the September quarter and we expect to
see detached house approvals show something similar. Unit
approvals are coming off a weak read and should be more
stable. Total approvals are expected to print 7% lower.

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Nov 5, Last: 0.5, Westpac f/c: 2.0
Market f/c: 1.0, Range: –1.0 to 2.5
Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August.
Recently, the trade balance has been oscillating between
small deficits and surpluses with significant volatility in the

underlying trade mix.
Swings in non-monetary gold, fuel and cars have all played
a large role, but the most eye-catching result from Aug was
the 79% surge in automatic data processing equipment (ADP)
imports, which captures the investment upswing related to
data centres.
For September, we expect an increase in the surplus as the
ADP import surge unwinds, though we are likely in store for
ongoing monthly volatility in trade for some time.
NZ: Sep Building Consents (%mth)

Nov 3, Last: +5.6%, Westpac f/c: Flat
Annual residential building consent numbers are currently at
a three year high, and we expect that they will remain firm in
the September update. However, monthly consent numbers
are showing signs of peaking, with falls in three of the last four
months. We’re watching to see if that trend has continued,
particularly given the ongoing softness in the housing
market.  In the non-residential space, we’ve seen softness

in the number of retail and office developments, but greater
resilience in the industrial and storage segment.
AUS: Sep Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Nov 4, Last: flat, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Market f/c: 0.2, Range: –0.5 to 0.8
The household spending indicator was unchanged in August,
annual growth easing to 6.8%yr. The detail showed a modest
rotation away from discretionary categories towards non-
discretionary.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker  showed a decent rise in

the September month although new vehicle sales (which are
not covered by the tracker but are in scope for the spending
indicator) look to have posted a decline. Overall we expect
the indicator to show a 0.5% decline in the September month.
Note that the update with also include Q3 estimates of real
spending – we expect this to show a 0.6%qtr gain with prices
up 1.2%qtr and nominal spend up 1.8%qtr.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 Labour Market Surveys (%)
Nov 4, Unemployment Rate, Last: 5.6, Westpac f/c: 5.5
LCI Private Sector, Last: 0.7, Westpac f/c: 0.5
We expect the unemployment rate to edge down to 5.5%
for the September quarter. Job numbers have been gaining
momentum in recent months, and now look to be enough to

outstrip population growth. However, the existing slack in the
labour market is likely to be keeping wage growth subdued,
despite cost-of-living pressures on workers. Our forecasts are
a little more positive overall than what the RBNZ assumed in
its September MPS forecasts.
US: Oct Employment Report
Nov 6, Payrolls, Last: 29k, Market f/c: 55k, WBC f/c: 75k
Nov 6, U/E rate, Last: 4.2%, Market f/c: 4.2%, WBC f/c: 4.3%
Nonfarm payrolls have shown considerable volatility of late,

July’s -10k followed by a 133k gain in August then a 29k
increase in September. However, the 3-month average of 51k
is likely a good guide on underlying momentum. In October, we
look for a 75k gain, although we expect some of that increase
to be offset by a modest reduction to the prior two months,
keeping the 3-month average around 50k.
While the participation rate has lifted in recent months, it
remains well below the average of 2025. Month-to-month, a

change either way is not guaranteed, but it seems likely that
the participation rate will trend higher in coming months. We
therefore expect the unemployment rate to edge up.

### Page 1

The ECB sent a clear message yesterday: a December hike is
likely. That is providing a more supportive backdrop for the euro
ahead of today’s 3Q GDP release. Meanwhile, resurgent AI-
related jitters and the upcoming US midterms are increasing
downside risks for the dollar, in our view, although a still-
hawkish Fed argues against major corrections

Frantisek Taborsky, Francesco Pesole and Chris Turner
We see some precautionary unwinding of net-long positions ahead of
Tuesday’s US midterm election as the main risk for the dollar today
USD: Midterm positioning may start to
kick in
Fresh pressure on tech stocks has triggered some safe-haven
buying in US Treasuries and weighed on the dollar as the front end
followed lower. Moves are contained in both equities and FX, but it’s
a reminder that the greenback still faces downside risks from
oscillating AI sentiment.

### Page 2

At the same time, whenever tech stocks don’t dominate headlines,
the dollar has had no trouble attracting buyers, and we don’t think
Wednesday’s FOMC changed that. Incidentally, yesterday’s US data
was hawkish-leaning, with 3Q growth slightly above consensus at
3.4% and core PCE at 0.3% month-on-month in September –
enough to keep the Federal Reserve on high alert.
To us, the main risk for the dollar today is some precautionary
unwinding of net-long positions ahead of Tuesday’s US midterm

elections. A Democratic sweep is trading around 68% in prediction
markets, and that could be seen as slightly negative for the US
dollar from a few angles, from higher risk of government shutdown
and gridlock on debt ceiling negotiations to potential damage to
already shaky AI-related sentiment. We’d be cautious in chasing
the USD upside in the next few days.
Today marks the end of the FOMC’s blackout period, and we’ll hear
from Thomas Barkin (neutral), Mary Daly (moderate dove) and

Alberto Musalem (moderate hawk). But we don’t feel there is much
members can add at this stage, as markets are already pricing in a
December hike and dovish comments look unwarranted given the
latest energy price developments.
Francesco Pesole
EUR: ECB to keep tightening going
The euro went on a small rollercoaster yesterday as the European
Central Bank held rates. The statement initially came across as
slightly dovish, with a reference to ﬁnancial conditions weighing on

inﬂation and growth. But President Christine Lagarde then delivered
a few hawkish remarks in the press conference that propped up the
euro.
Lagarde said a hike was discussed at the meeting and hinted that
the direction of travel remains towards further tightening. She also
gave no indication that French bonds are prompting any dovish
rethink at the ECB. Finally, the customary tip to the media a few
hours later indicated that oﬃcials are “ready” to raise rates in

### Page 3

December. That was another clear endorsement of rate
expectations and has left the euro better supported.
The side note was Lagarde’s resignation, eﬀective 7 February, which
brings forward the announcement of her successor by a few
months. Pablo Hernández de Cos (dove) and Klaas Knot (hawk) are
the frontrunners. Should the former be announced, it could bring
some downside risks to the euro.
Today, we’ll take a look at third-quarter GDP data in the eurozone.

We expect a slightly stronger-than-consensus 0.3% quarter-on-
quarter print. France will be monitored closely: we expect 0%
growth (consensus 0.1%), and a negative ﬁgure here could cause
some fresh widening in spreads.
We see a decent chance of EUR/USD re-exploring the 1.120-1.1250
area, as the dollar faces downside risks related to AI stocks and US
midterms.
Francesco Pesole
JPY: BoJ stays hawkish as CPI target hit
The Bank of Japan left the policy interest rate at 1.25% today and,

for the ﬁrst time, signalled that its 2% inﬂation target had been hit.
Running through both the statement and the new Outlook report
were three themes that the BoJ felt could all contribute to upside
price risks: energy prices, AI demand and the weak yen. In that
report, growth forecasts were left around 0.7-0.8% (near or above
potential), while CPI forecasts were revised slightly higher.
Markets are still debating whether the BoJ will hike next at the

December or January meeting, but remain conﬁdent that the
policy rate will be heading up to the 2.00% area next summer.
The BoJ also gave a nod to rate hikes overseas and presumably will
be aware of the risks of a weaker yen should it not track the Fed and
ECB with a rate hike in December. We now think the BoJ will hike in
December, with a likely follow-up in March/April.
USD/JPY is a little lower today on this hawkish hold – although the
dollar is a little lower across the board. We see it staying bid

### Page 4

towards the top of a broad 155-160 range and then turning lower

Chris Turner
CEE: The relief rally won't last long
The CEE region will see its ﬁrst important data release of the week
today, with inﬂation ﬁgures from Poland and GDP data from the
Czech Republic and Hungary for the third quarter. We estimate that
Poland’s October inﬂation fell from 4.1% to 3.8% year-on-year,
largely due to the government’s introduction of fuel price caps.

However, the breakdown should be somewhat more hawkish than
the headline ﬁgures, with core inﬂation rising from 3.1% to 3.3%.
In Hungary, we expect a slowdown to 0.2% quarter-on-quarter,
alongside an acceleration in year-on-year growth to 1.7%.
Conversely, in the Czech Republic, we see an acceleration to 0.6%
quarter-on-quarter, but a slowdown to 1.7% year-on-year. The
National Bank of Hungary projects 1.6%, while the Czech National
Bank’s August forecast anticipated 2.2%; however, the Q2 ﬁgures

already came in 0.4 percentage points below expectations.
The Fed and ECB meetings brought some stabilisation to CEE
currencies, in line with expectations, and we saw a rally in the forint
and the zloty – the ﬁrst in a week. However, the market suggests
that this relief will not last long.
Speculation regarding a resumption of US attacks on Iran, new
highs in energy prices, and a return to a sell-oﬀ in the stock market
are bringing a "risk-oﬀ" mood back into play – exerting pressure on

CEE currencies, just as we anticipated. Our bearish bias remains,
and the zloty appears the most vulnerable here, unless inﬂation
surprises on the upside this morning.
Frantisek Taborsky

Document 333: Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

10
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AN EXPORT-DRIVEN RECOVERY
Growth, which began in the fourth quarter of 2025, gained momentum in the first half of 2026. This momentum should
continue, with expected growth of 1.1% in 2026 and 1.3% in 2027. In the short term, the main driver will still be exports,
particularly to Europe, which will contribute to restructuring industry. The impact of investment plans is expected to

strengthen in 2027, while domestic demand is likely to remain constrained by inflation (2.7% in 2026 and 2.6 % in 2027),
which will weigh on purchasing power. Fiscal stimulus and structural reforms are expected to continue to support growth.
However, the rise in the deficit and debt levels means that the increase in long-term interest rates observed so far is mostly
structural.
A RETURN TO GROWTH DRIVEN BY EXTERNAL TRADE
The first half of 2026 confirms the rebound in growth (0.4% and then

0.3% q/q in Q1 and Q2). This growth is driven by foreign trade (contribu-
tions from net exports of +0.8 pp and +0.2 pp, respectively, with +0.9 pp
on average coming from exports and -0.4 pp from imports), following
two years of contracting exports, particularly those to Europe (see be -
low). The increase in public expenditure ( see our analysis ) reflects
the implementation of investment plans, with the multiplier effect on
growth of these plans expected to increase.

In the second half of the year, growth is expected to remain robust,
pushing the annual average to 1.1% in 2026 (following 0.3% in 2025).
Exports are set to remain the main driver, thanks to continued strong
European demand, which is compensating for domestic demand that
remains sluggish. Growth of private consumption will, in fact, remain
subdued by energy inflation. Business investment is expected to be
driven by dynamic sectors (defence, technology equipment and aeros -

pace), which would limit the impact of rising interest rates.
In 2027, growth (1.3%) would be based more heavily on domestic de -
mand, with greater spillover effects from investment plans and a likely
recovery in household consumption (following a peak in inflation in
Q1 2027). At the same time, external demand is expected to remain
strong, but its contribution to growth would be lower than in 2026.
This is because the stronger growth in domestic demand would stimu-

late imports to a greater extent.
INDUSTRY IS BENEFITING FROM AI INVESTMENT AND INTRA-EUROPEAN
MOMENTUM
The business climate in industry improved significantly at the start of
the second half of the year (the manufacturing PMI has risen by 4 points
since May, reaching a four-year high in August). New export orders have
even reached their highest level since early 2022. These positive signs
put into perspective the risks associated with the summer’s climate

events (heatwaves and the historic drought on the Rhine), which dis -
rupted industrial activity and river transport.
Since early 2025, output in some sectors has rebounded, particularly in
the electronics sector (+6.4 %). Manufacturers of electrical equipment,
as well as IT and electronics firms, are benefiting in particular from
the wave of investment in data centres and defence. If this momentum
continues, it could offset some of the losses recorded in the automotive

and chemicals sectors, which have both seen the sharpest falls in out-
put since late 2017 (-12.8% and -19.4%, respectively).
The sustained increase in military spending on medical technologies
and robotics across Europe is driving a restructuring of the interme -
diate-goods sector. Companies exposed to struggling sectors are gra -
dually turning towards these new markets. However, this reorientation
is not fully offsetting the loss of historical markets.

The rebound in German industry is also being fuelled by European
demand. While exports to China (-12.7% YTD in July) and the United
States (-1.5%) have certainly continued to fall, exports to a number of
European Union countries have risen sharply, particularly in Southern
and Central Europe. This shift is benefiting machinery and transport
equipment (47.6% of goods exports, up 20.5% since January 2025) in
particular. We expect this momentum to continue in 2027, creating a

virtuous cycle that reinforces European growth.
A STAGNANT LABOUR MARKET
The situation in the German labour market has stabilised over the
past several quarters, but it is not yet showing signs of improvement.
The unemployment rate is shifting relatively little (4% in November;
+0.2 pp y/y) and remains below the Eurozone average (6.4%). Falling
employment in the manufacturing sector (-0.6% since Q4 2019) is offset
by an increase in the services sector (+0.9%). Nevertheless, the latest

business climate surveys suggest that the situation may stabilise. The
gradual retraining of the workforce in struggling sectors is helping to
meet some of the needs arising from new investment.
Generally speaking, in the longer term, pension reform should support
the labour supply. As a matter of fact, it will help to increase the num-
ber of workers gradually, notably through the phasing out of early reti-
rement at 63 and the indexing of the retirement age to life expectancy

from 2032 onwards.
GERMANY
Growth and inflation

world
Although the unemployment rate is set to remain low, wage growth
would remain limited, particularly in 2026, as negotiated wages will
offset inflation to a lesser extent than they will in 2027. The planned
increases in the minimum wage (+8% in 2026 and +5% in 2027, to
EUR 14.6 per hour), significant as they are, will have little knock-on

effect on the rest of the wage scale.
PURCHASING POWER CONTINUES TO BE SQUEEZED BY HIGH INFLATION
Wage purchasing power is expected to fall further in 2026, having
already lost 4 pp since 2022, before stabilising in 2027. The reduction
in income tax (by EUR 10 billion, or 0.2% of GDP), which will come into
force next January, is also expected to support the disposable income of
low- and middle-income households.
Inflation has been rising again since the start of the summer and is

expected to remain above 2% throughout our forecast horizon. It has
accelerated since June (+0.5 pp to +2.9% y/yin August) driven by a fur -
ther rise in energy prices (+6.7 pp to +9.4% y/y), while core inflation re-
mains stable at +2.6%. From spring 2027 onwards, the expected easing
in energy prices (particularly gas prices) should bring down inflation.
However, this decline will be limited by the inflationary effects of the
wider roll-out of investment plans, as well as by the expected rise in

food prices.
This inflation, which has been structurally higher since the onset of the
2022 energy shock, will weigh on household consumption. Furthermore,
German households’ propensity to save remains high by international
standards (with a net balance 15 points higher on average over the
last ten years, according to the European Commission), which is also
weighing on their consumption.
FISCAL STIMULUS CONTINUES
After recording a public deficit of -3% of GDP in 2025, we anticipate a

further deterioration in the public balance to -4% in 2026, then -4.2%
in 2027. The primary deficit is projected to reach -2.8% of GDP in 2026
(after -1.9% in 2025) before narrowing slightly to -2.7% in 2027. At the
same time, the interest burden is expected to rise gradually from 1.1%
of GDP in 2025 to 1.5% in 2027. This combination will result in a rapid
rise in the debt-to-GDP ratio, from 62.6% of GDP in 2025 to 66.1% in
2026 and then 69.7% in 2027, before approaching 85% by the end of the

decade.
The yield on the 10-year Bund rose above 3.5% on 14 September, its
highest level since 2007. In addition to the increase in issues linked to
extra-budgetary funds, inflationary pressures are also contributing to
this rise. A gradual easing of inflation next year would make a mode -
rate easing of sovereign yields possible in 2027. However, yields would
remain high, at 3.2% in Q4 2027, suggesting that the bulk of the rise is
structural.
At the same time, the government is continuing to implement its pro -

gramme of structural reforms, including measures to support business
investment, health-insurance reform, pension reform and an overhaul
of the tax system. A number of measures have come into force, par -
ticularly accelerated depreciation for business investment until 2027,
which will be followed by a reduction in corporation tax from 2028.
Administrative simplification is also set to lead to an overhaul of the
tax system from January 2027 1. The health-insurance reform has been

passed, while the pension reform is still under discussion, with expecta-
tions that it will be adopted by the end of the year.
1  The main measure will be a reduction of EUR 10 billion per year in income tax for individuals on low incomes, funded in part by an increase in taxes on high earners. The bill will
be submitted to Parliament in the autumn, before being implemented from 2027.
Article completed on 14 September 2026
Lucie Barette
lucie.barette@bnpparibas.com

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
2
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
• The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index* has edged down slightly since
late September, slipping 0.9pts to 160.6
in the week ended October 17. Despite
this, growth in activity continues to track
a robust pace with quarterly momentum

running at close to 2%qtr and annual
growth at 6%yr.
• Some of this reflects revisions. Initial
figures on card activity can see changes
in subsequent weeks due to information
lags. While these are usually small,
the cumulative impact can sometimes
be material. In this case, revisions have
lifted the index in late September by
2.5pts and the 4wk trend growth rate at
the time from 0.2% to 0.6%. We will be
back-testing regular revisions to assess
whether there is a systematic bias that

can be adjusted for in future.
• The revised profile shows quarterly
growth accelerating steadily since
June, posting a 1.9%qtr gain for the
September quarter and tracking a
2.1%qtr pace in the latest week. This is
back near the robust pace seen in late
2024 and early 2025.
• The monthly and weekly reads show
a softer 0.2%mth rise in August was
followed by a solid 0.8%mth gain in
September that has largely carried into
the first two weeks of October (the latest

observation tracking a 0.4%mth pace).
• The detail shows gains are not just
due to higher fuel prices with strong
growth in discretionary services and
international transactions over the last
quarter and steady growth in essential
services as well. To the extent that the
weekly and monthly data is showing
some renewed squeeze from higher fuel
prices, this is showing through most
clearly in discretionary goods, which
have seen activity decline over the last

six weeks, with discretionary services
still posting positive, albeit slower
growth. Quarterly growth in non-fuel
card activity is tracking around 1.4%qtr.
• Official September quarter figures on
inflation and household spending due
in the coming weeks should confirm the
spending momentum and shed more
light on the extent to which it is due to
prices or volumes.
“... quarterly momentum
running at close to 2%qtr ...”

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
3
• Chart 2 shows how quarterly growth
in the Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index compares to growth in nominal
consumption as reported in the national
accounts. Growth has shown a clear
acceleration since late June, lifting to
1.9%qtr in the September quarter and

running at 2.1%qtr in the latest week.
Weekly reads in early October have been
the strongest since July 2025.
• Only some of this reflects a price-
driven rise in fuel spend. The unwind of
temporary fuel excise tax cuts in August
and more recent surge in global energy
prices has seen local pump prices push
back above $2.40/litre locally, up from
$1.56/litre in late June.
• Excluding fuel, quarterly growth in card
activity has moderated, but only slightly,

from 1.9%qtr in August to 1.8% in
September with the latest week tracking
a 1.4% quarterly pace. The impact is
more pronounced in monthly terms with
a –0.4%mth decline in August, muted
+0.2% gain in September and subdued
+0.1% monthly pace in the latest week
(c.f. total: –0.4%; +0.2% and +0.1%).
• The 1.9%qtr rise in nominal terms and an
expected price rise of 1.1%qtr point to a
real, inflation-adjusted gain of 0.8%qtr,
in line with Westpac’s forecasts. ABS Q3

CPI figures are due shortly after we go to
press with the Q3 household spending
indicator due Nov 4 and GDP due Dec 2.
